  2. Becoming climate sentinels: Ecological perplexities and technoscientific entrainments in South Korea
    Sumin Myung · online 2026-10-13
    摘要

    In South Korea, forest scientists refashion themselves as “climate sentinels” as they confront subtle yet disquieting shifts in plant seasonality brought about by climate change. Unlike traditional sentinels, who watch for external threats from a frontier, climate sentinels face ecological anomalies and surprises—such as untimely flowering—that have permeated everyday spaces. To register such ecological perplexities, they engage in technoscientific entrainments: deliberate, disciplinary, and laborious coordinations of human observers, trees, protocols, institutions, and modular devices into a shared rhythm of attention. These experimental processes redistribute the traditional embodied technique of mokch’ŭk (eye observation) across nationwide phenological observation networks while reanimating the ecological significance of plant seasonality. For these sentinels, confronting climate change requires attending not only to satellites and heavily computed models but also to the distributed, more‐than‐human experiments through which ecological perplexities are made sensible on the ground.

  3. To flirt, or not to flirt, that is the ethnographic question
    Katarzyna Wojnicka · online 2026-10-07
    摘要

    Despite the title suggesting a focus on contemporary dating practices, this commentary is not yet another take on (post)#MeToo dating practices. Instead, it is an attempt to add my two cents to the discussion on flirting as a methodological strategy in ethnographic, anthropological, and sociological research, which is the focus of Anna Jochymek's “I Got Caught in My Own Trap.” In this highly engaging article, the author reflects on her fieldwork experiences as part of a research project in the UK on far-right activists with a migration background. Many of them are of Polish origin, and so is the author. This made her a perfect person to conduct the interviews, since she was immediately defined as a specific type of insider, which in research involving migrants is usually (but not always) considered an asset (e.g., Pustulka et al., 2019). Moreover, most far-right activists in the UK (and elsewhere) are cismen (Ralph-Morrow, 2022), and the author is a ciswoman. Their gender identities played a pivotal role in the fieldwork dynamic, in which the flirtation, initiated by one of the research participants, is the main plot of the article, touching on questions of agency, risk, and security during fieldwork, emotional labor, and ethics, as well as on third-wave feminism. In other words, the article adds some new perspectives to the ongoing discussions on the positionality of female (and male) academics in research on masculinities and (with) men, developed over three decades within critical studies on men and masculinities (Pini & Pease, 2013). Jochymek's article is an interesting addition to this scholarship, given that flirting during fieldwork has already been a prominent issue (Kaspar & Landolt, 2016; Rodríguez-Dorans, 2018; Wojnicka, 2020) and is discussed mostly in the context of benefits and risks associated with the practices of flirting with the (mostly male) research participants. And there are many of both. Among the benefits, one can list access to participants, which is usually easier for female researchers, as exemplified in Jochymek's article and elsewhere (e.g., Mügge, 2013; Wojnicka, 2020). The relative ease of discussing sensitive topics, such as intimate relationships and family issues, also appears to be linked to the dynamic between female researchers and male research participants. But the dark sides of flirting include the risk of sexual harassment, the potential undermining of the researcher's credibility, as mentioned by Jochymek, and, not least, the difficulty of leaving the field. A particularly compelling aspect of the article is its engagement with the concept of agency. Jochymek discloses that her decision to employ “flirting methodology” was intentional, and drawing on Kaspar and Landolt (2016), she frames it as an exercise of agency in using her gender, sexuality, and body within research practice. She articulates how this choice influenced both her fieldwork and her academic positionality. What we know far less about is the impact of flirting on research participants. While the author notes that the participant initiated the flirtation, little is known about the consequences for participants when this data-collection approach is used. Existing literature suggests that many interviewees, particularly migrants, may experience loneliness (Wojnicka, forthcoming). The chance to have meaningful conversations with an attentive female researcher may be difficult for them to decline. Yet questions remain about their experiences after fieldwork concludes: How do they process the emotions generated during these interactions? How do they cope with the inevitable separation when the researcher returns to her personal and professional life, while participants remain in their previous circumstances? As a female researcher who has conducted qualitative studies with men for over 20 years, I've always encountered the flirting issue. Although most of my research participants, who today can be numbered in the hundreds, did not flirt with me, in almost every project I have been involved in, some men have tried to do so. As a young scholar, I was initially shocked by these situations; then I got used to them; then I started developing strategies aimed at minimizing the opportunities for flirting (which rarely even worked). Eventually, I wrote papers in which I address “flirting methodology” and its pros and cons (Wojnicka, 2020). I choose to avoid flirting in fieldwork for several reasons. Part of this is motivated by the risks discussed above, but at the current stage of my career, I am mostly concerned about the research participants and how flirting affects them and their lives after the fieldwork ends. From my practice, I know that for many of them, meeting a researcher who listens can feel like an opportunity to start a new relationship. This is especially evident for single men, with whom I usually interact because of the specificity of my research projects. Even years after the interview, I sometimes receive messages from them asking how I am, telling me what they are up to, and sharing what bothers them in life. And this happens despite my decision to avoid flirting at any cost. What happens to research participants when flirting is part of the data-collection process remains unknown and should not be ignored. Hence, whether to flirt is a critical question for female ethnologists, anthropologists, and sociologists researching men and masculinities, and it should be considered the next time we establish contact with a potential research participant.

  4. Monsters of finance: An anthropology of horror in agrarian Paraguay
    Caroline E. Schuster · online 2026-10-07
    摘要

    An anthropology of horror can serve as a vernacular critique of financial speculation. Drawing on fieldwork in San Pedro, Paraguay, I track horror across three registers: pombero tricksters; plata yvyguy (buried money) as a founding myth of postwar finance; and obligations binding sesame farmers to monsters, patrones , and creditors. Horror—a critical ontology and distinctive wonder—arises when obligation becomes undecidable: when one cannot tell whether the forces manipulating time and the future are persons who stand in relation, or how far the liability extends. Paraguay has produced this condition twice, first under the Stroessner dictatorship (1954–89), with its network of civilian informants, and later under climate finance running through the same clientelist orders. As Martha Blache's 1982 study of Guarani folklore helps us understand, finance bears rather than subsumes the moral arrangements of prestation, and horror is a native theorization of speculation rather than a response to an external capitalist threat.

  6. The politics of evidence making: AE interviews Courtney Addison and Sameena Mulla
    Courtney Addison, Sameena Mulla, Susanna Trnka, L. L. Wynn, Jesse Hession Grayman · online 2026-10-06
    摘要

    What is evidence, and how is it constituted across different scientific, legal, political and social domains? Courts, medical clinics, scientific enterprises, public constituencies, as well as academic journals, come to define, adjudicate, and thus constitute evidence through a variety of openly and covertly political processes, as Sameena Mulla and Courtney Addison discuss. Reflecting on fieldwork they have conducted on a range of topics—including sexual assault intervention, forensic evidence collection, court sentencing, genomic medicine, and conservation—they suggest what kinds of questions anthropologists might want to ask in thinking through not only the nature of evidence making but also its impact on social communities. They also highlight what is at stake in the shaping and contestation of evidence, including justice, social cohesion and environmental futures.

  8. “I got caught in my own trap”: Methodological risks and advantages of women's positionality in researching the Far Right
    Anna Jochymek · online 2026-10-01
    摘要

    For women ethnographers, research involving predominantly male far‐right activists can involve many challenges—ethical, methodological, and emotional. Drawing on interviews and long‐term participant observation, I reflect on my fieldwork with Polish far‐right activists in the UK. In particular, I consider how this work demanded that I continually negotiate methodological access, gendered power, emotional self‐management, and my opposition to my interlocutors’ politics. In this critical self‐reflection, three key questions emerge: (1) How can researchers cultivate empathy and navigate professional regard for far‐right activists without compromising their ethical stance? (2) What are the ethical and methodological implications of using flirtation as a strategy in the field? (3) How can third‐wave feminist understandings of agency shape the emotional experience and practices of ethnographic fieldwork in male‐dominated far‐right milieus?

  17. Criminalizing hate speech in the Balkans: Rethinking endgame analysis and the threshold problem in the prosecution of speech crimes
    Jordan Kiper · online 2026-09-30
    摘要

    When does speech lose its legal protection and become criminally sanctionable because it causes violence? Among legal scholars, this is known as the “threshold problem,” and in international criminal law (ICL), the threshold is defined according to recent speech‐crime trials at the International Criminal Tribunal for the Former Yugoslavia. Yet, as borne out in ethnographic interview data, survivors and former combatants of the Yugoslav Wars disagree with ICL's approach to speech crimes. Judges in international tribunals deploy what I call endgame analysis , in which they consider the threshold of violent speech only at the inflection point when violence immediately and directly follows speech. In contrast, survivors and combatants assert that atrocity crimes resulted in part from the longer‐term accumulation of ethnic slurs and wartime misinformation enunciated by political and cultural authorities, both before and during the Yugoslav Wars. Challenging recent jurisprudence, they call for these former authorities to be held accountable for the violent, cumulative effect of their speech.

  19. Contemplative radicals: Navigating ethics and politics with Israeli engaged dharma practitioners
    Ori Mautner · online 2026-09-30
    摘要

    Engaged Dharma Israel (EDI), a radical‐left group, combines Buddhist‐inspired contemplation with anti‐occupation activism in solidarity with Palestinians. For EDI, ethical self‐cultivation and political resistance are mutually sustaining commitments, yet their relationship demands ongoing, sometimes anxious work. Participants challenge both dharma practitioners who pursue self‐fashioning without political engagement and radical‐left activists who pursue political opposition without systematic ethical reflection, insisting each orientation is incomplete without the other. Nimrod, an everyday exemplar of EDI's approach, realizes its plural, sometimes conflicting values. Navigating risks of complicity, hostility, burnout, and physical harm, he demonstrates how participants work out the relationship between self‐cultivation and resistance, holding their respective values in productive tension and transforming them into embodied virtues. Tracing this navigation offers a generative response to a recurring anthropological question—how to analytically relate the ethical and the political—by ethnographically comparing the ways people connect, separate, or sequence them beyond any single theoretical configuration.

    What drives American men to look for wives overseas, and what does this tell us about contemporary gender relations?Julia Meszaros's ethnography is not the first to investigate the "mail order   24. The Eurabia myth: Countercolonization and masculine fragility in France By MehammedAmadeus Mack. Minneapolis: University of Minnesota Press, 2025. 416 pp.
    Henry Maher · online 2026-09-19
    摘要

    Once confined to the extreme political fringes, the racist conspiracy known as the Great Replacement Theory has become a defining force in contemporary far-right politics, motivating racist movements from Europe to North America, Australia, and New Zealand.The Eurabia Myth, by Mehammed Amadeus Mack, returns to the origin point of the Great Replacement, French politics, and situates the GRT as only the latest iteration of the long co-constitutive interactions between the Christian and Islamic world.Situated at the intersection of ethnic studies, masculinity studies, and French studies, the book adopts a broad scope of analysis, combining historiographical analysis of medieval Europe with analysis of speculative dystopian fiction, psychoanalytic insights, anthropological analysis of contemporary far-right movements, and interviews with the descendants of immigrants demonized by the GRT.The opening chapter describes the apocalyptic narrative of "Eurabia" constructed by the French Far Right.According to this worldview, white French society is being "replaced" by waves of Muslim invaders, resulting in the loss of French secular and Republican principles and the ultimate suicide of white French culture.A great melancholy imbues these narratives, in which hatred is turned not just toward the immigrant outsider but also toward the French self, whose cowardice and weakness in the face of invasion has allegedly allowed its impending destruction.In this context, Mack introduces us to the bizarre appropriation of anti-colonial language and politics by the French Far Right, which, jealous of the righteousness of postcolonial struggle, claims to be itself a minority indigenous population facing cultural genocide at the hands of invaders.The claim of reverse colonization is one example of the broader psychoanalytic phenomenon Mack terms "adversarial becoming."Adversarial becoming describes the "tendency of being molded by one's sworn enemy and evolving into 'him.'All rights reserved, including rights for text and data mining and training of artificial intelligence technologies or similar technologies.

    摘要

    In recent decades, reconciliation has come to dominate settler state discourses and policies across the globe.Weiss's Irreconcilable serves as a vital and timely treatise on "the relationship between the settler colonial erasure of reconciliation and the ways in which Indigenous Peoples refuse to be reconciled to their dispossession or even their outright elimination" (p.3).There is a moment in the introduction when Weiss makes it clear that he will provide no solutions to settler colonial relations in and beyond Canada.This, as he asserts, is precisely the point-that "the desire for resolution is at the heart of the era of reconciliation" (p.18).By foregrounding irreconcilability, the book enables rich analyses of the complex realities from which Indigenous communities are enacting their political futures.It offers cultural anthropologists an incisive analysis of Indigenous-state relations, one whose theory is not divorced from political realities.Irreconcilable draws on an ethnographic sensibility to move across scales in ways that bounded fieldwork could not sustain.In particular, its depth of engagement with critical Native studies scholarship makes it essential reading for any anthropologist.There are books whose chapters are so disparate in method and topic that they struggle to hold together.This is not one of those books.Weiss's chapters are notably varied and span legal history, ethnography, cultural studies, political theory, semiotics, performance studies, museum studies, and ritual analysis.And yet, remarkably, the center never ceases to hold.This variety only makes the book's argument stronger, by demonstrating the extent to which reconciliation-which, as Weiss notes, is "always so-called reconciliation" (p.88)-has soaked through settler colonial practices and institutions.At its broadest level, Irreconcilable is a dialogue between two frameworks.The first is generative refusal-drawing on influential work by Mohawk anthropologist Audra Simpson and Mississauga Nishnaabeg scholar Leanne Betasamosake All rights reserved, including rights for text and data mining and training of artificial intelligence technologies   7. 「自然的なもの」を考える
    浅田 直規 · pp. 005-024
    摘要

    本稿は、東欧ルーマニアを調査地として、食に関わる様々な場面で人々が語る「自然さ( natural )」や「健康さ( sănătos )」の意味について論じている。ルーマニアでは近年、有機栽培や自然食品消費が拡大しているが、人々が語る「自然さ」は公的な認証制度に還元しえない広がりを含んでいる。そこで、本稿では文化人類学における食研究とサブスタンス論を手掛かりに、ルーマニアの食に関わるいくつかの場面を民族誌的に描きつつ、「自然さ」や「健康さ」という言葉によって彼らが語ろうとしているものについて迫っていく。 本稿が対象とする食に関わるいくつかの場面とは、人々が囲む食卓であり、調理の風景であり、食材とその飼育・生育であり、食材が育つ土地と人々との関わり合いである。そして、それぞれの場面で人々が実践し、想像するものについて記述し、それらを並置して描き出す。 このように全体論的なサブスタンスの流れとしてルーマニアの食を見ていくことで、ルーマニアの人々にとっての「自然さ」とは、その流れの中に適切に位置づけられることであることを示す。そして、人々が「自然」だと想像するものを、自然や人為に還元しえない「自然的なもの(the natural)」として捉えることを提起する。

  8. 「自然的なもの」を考える : ルーマニアの食に見るサブスタンスの循環に関する考察
    浅田 直規 · pp. 5-24
  9. 隠岐民謡の知識をめぐるつながりと分離
    佐々木 悠 · pp. 025-045
    摘要

    本稿では、島根県隠岐の島町の民謡を事例に、その知識の社会的布置を明らかにする。これを通じて、知識の獲得がどのような関係性への参与をもたらすかを考察する。 知識人類学では、知識の獲得が社会関係への参加と一体であることが指摘されてきた。とりわけ、地域社会に偏差を有しながら遍在し、個々人に分有されながら実演を通じて変容する民俗芸能の知識は、地域社会における関係性と密接に結びついている。他方で関係論的人類学では、関係がつながりと分離の双方を含むと論じられている。しかしこれらを架橋する形で、知識の獲得がどのような関係性を生み出すかは、詳述されてこなかった。 隠岐民謡の知識を取り巻く関係性は、島嶼性に左右される交流の多寡、民謡の担い手としての島民と外部者、宴席での共同性と個々の担い手による差異化といった、様々な水準におけるつながりと分離の重層的な絡み合いである。こうした関係性のなかで、隠岐民謡の知識を構成する「隠岐」や「規範的実践」は、フラクタル的な空洞を成している。隠岐民謡の知識の獲得は、実践共同体における不在の中心への接近であり、その姿は個々の実演で初めて開示される。

  10. 序
    太田 好信 · pp. 046-057
    摘要

    The critical voices of the Indigenous Peoples in Japan as they have surfaced recently in the discussions of the draft version of the "Ethical Code for Ainu Studies" demand anthropologists to revisit the disciplinary past of its colonial regime. In responding to a call for renewed engagement with the colonial past, the articles grouped together under the theme, "The Paradox of Knowledge in a Colonial Regime," examine complex issues of colonial legacy employing a group of concepts that traverse multiple temporalities: for example, implication, inheritance, memory, and responsibilities. All of these concepts are also multi-directional in a sense that they allow anthropologists to build a new relationship among the colonially divided by constructing coalition in difference: call it a "creative praxis." Through historical, politico-philosophical, and ethnographic reflections on various discourses and practices in Japan, they try envisioning a new path for the future of anthropological knowledge that acknowledges the burden of the past without becoming its victim.

  11. 応答するエスノグラフィ
    飯嶋 秀治 · pp. 058-078
    摘要

    現在を生きる私たちが、何を選択し、どう行為するのかで、その結果が私たちに問われること、これが責任問題である。しかし、過去の先人の行為が、現在を生きる私たちに問われることもある。その問いかけにどう応えるのか、過去が自己の形成に寄与してきた関係をどう捉えるのか、それをどのように現在の私たちの行為につなげるのか。本稿では、被災地を巡って問われたこうした問いかけに、「連累」関係を見出し、試行錯誤しながら関係の形成を試みてきた実践を紹介し、出遭ったところから始まるエスノグラフィの在り方を提案する。それは過去の研究者の学知への批判からはじまり、依然として社会的正義が果たされない現場で、複数の多元的な可能性を抱えたものとして文化人類学を捉え直すことでもある。

  12. 応答するエスノグラフィ : 異方向の連累のなかでの試行錯誤
    飯嶋 秀治 · pp. 58-78
  13. 「自己否定する主体」への創造的実践
    伊地知 紀子 · pp. 079-096
    摘要

    本論文では、過去の責任に関する新たな「私たち」という回路の設定はいかにして可能かという問いを考察する。「大阪コリアタウン歴史資料館」の設立と運営に関わる私自身の経験を事例とし、ローカルな知と社会の関係が複雑に絡み合う場の創造と変容を検討する。参照軸とするのは、朝鮮史研究者・梶村秀樹による実践の軌跡である。 梶村は、1960年代以降、在日朝鮮人の調査研究と現場の往還関係を構築し、日本の朝鮮植民地支配という歴史的不正義と責任への向き合い方を模索した。ただ、梶村に代表される人びとを駆動してきた、歴史性に対して真正面からイデオロギー的に取り組む「歴史認識的に正しい」主張が、ある面において直接過去に関与してこなかった世代の一部からの忌避や反発を招いたことは事実だ。一方で歴史性を全く欠落させ韓流現象と接続し過去を消し去る「歴史修正主義」も勃興してきた。これらを同時に克服するための苦難の実践(実験)が資料館の挑戦であった。 本特集のテーマにかかる論考では、関与する個人の属性に伴う歴史性、知的資源の有無、認識の正当性といった諸指標によって「アイデンティティの政治」が構築され、ともすれば、これによる分断線が強固となる。本論文では、こうした分断線の形成と輻輳を可能な限り紐解くよう努め、植民地主義の過去を引き受けつつ未来にむけた創造的実践を展開しながら、分断線を修復する「私たち」の創出可能性について考察する。

  14. 「自己否定する主体」への創造的実践 : 大阪コリアタウン歴史資料館における「私たち」とは誰か
    伊地知 紀子 · pp. 79-96
  15. 過去の責任は誰がどう負うのか
    松田 素二 · pp. 097-116
    摘要

    本論は、2024年に日本文化人類学会が発出した「アイヌ民族研究に関する声明」を起点として、文化人類学における「過去の責任」とは誰がどのように引き受けるべきものなのかを検討する。声明は未来に向けた責任を掲げたが、研究者による遺骨盗掘や民具窃取など、過去の不正行為に対する文化人類学という学知、その学知共同体、さらにはそれに属する人類学者の具体的責任の所在やその果たし方は曖昧にされたままである。本論はこの問題を、日本国家による戦争責任・植民地支配責任の免責構造や人類学知が植民地主義や戦争に深く関与してきた歴史の検討を参照軸として分析する。各国の学会や国家が発出してきた謝罪や遺憾の表明は、倫理的反省を表明しつつも、構造的責任の承認を回避する点で共通している。本論は、個別の行為者の違法性を超えて、学知と制度に刻印された構造的問題として過去の不正義を捉え直し、被害者・当事者との対話を通じて責任を引き受ける社会的実践の可能性を探る。過去の反省にとどまらず、非当事者世代である現在の研究者が、学知のあり方そのものを問い直し、共生と和解に資する人類学の条件を深く考える手がかりを提供することを目的とする。

  16. 過去との向き合いから「遺骨返還プロジェクト」へ
    太田 好信 · pp. 117-135
    摘要

    「アイヌ民族に関する研究倫理指針(案)」をめぐる議論のなかで(マスコミでは「遺骨研究の道を開きたい学術界と、それを危惧するアイヌ民族」という対立図式で捉えられている)「遺骨問題」が浮き彫りになった。本論文は、この問題の解決にむけた「遺骨返還プロジェクト」を構想するにあたり、そのプロジェクトの中心になりうる概念を提案する。その概念とは、「遺骨問題」において敵対関係にある両者をともに植民地主義的力からの「生存者」と捉えなおす考えである。この概念を基盤にした「遺骨返還プロジェクト」は、植民地主義が植民者と被植民者の両方をつくりだし、両方を非人間化したという認識のもと、両者がともに人間性を回復するための連帯を可能にする。加害者、被害者というカテゴリーは帰責モデルに基づき、分断と対立を深めても、連帯への道を開く目的には適さない。「誰がやったのか」という責任追及は過去を志向している。「どうして起きたのか」という問いは、「遺骨問題」を植民地主義的力に結びつけ、その解消にむけた連帯と協働という未来への道を開く。「生存者」という考えは、過去と向き合いつつも、未来を志向する連帯へとつらなる概念といえる。最後に、植民地主義的力からの「生存者」たちが、哀悼可能性の平等な分配を求める「遺骨返還プロジェクト」をともにつくりだす作業(創造的実践)は、社会のなかに知を実装する一具体例となることを示す。

  17. 過去との向き合いから「遺骨返還プロジェクト」へ : 責任、連帯、植民地主義的力
    太田 好信 · pp. 117-135
  18. ムダによる翻訳
    高田 洋平 · pp. 136-146
    摘要

    This article examines how material objects participate in the formation of contested urban space through an ethnographic study of muda , a cylindrical bamboo stool used on the streets of Kathmandu, Nepal. Drawing on Actor–Network Theory(ANT), it traces how muda has been translated across heterogeneous networks involving caste communities, the king, prisons, development discourse, and urban markets. Focusing on Asan, a historic market in the old city, the analysis shows that muda functions not merely as a seat but as a mediator shaping bodily postures, spatial practices, and ways of being in public space among regulated street vendors. By connecting ANT with the concept of ikata (ways of being-in-place), the article demonstrates how muda mediates forms of ikata at the boundary between mobility and everyday life. Compatible with concrete and human bodies, muda generates an intermediate realm— awai —by enabling practices that neither cross nor dissolve that boundary. The study thus contributes to debates on materiality and public space.

비교문화연구 (Cross-Cultural Studies)

Vol. 32, No. 1 · 2026-06-30 · 12 篇 · 서울대학교 비교문화연구소 · 문화인류학 · Peer-reviewed (double-blind not verified)
  1. The Reconfiguration of Mikea Subsistence and the Logic of the Forest : A Reinterpretation through a Combined Model of the Ideal Free Distribution and the Allee Effect

  2. Accidental Madam, Power in Discomfort : Korean Expat wives and the Affective Encounters with Migrant Domestic Workers in Bangkok
    Heejin KIM · pp. 73-111
    摘要

    이 연구는 태국 방콕 주재원 배우자가 가사노동자를 고용하며 겪는 ‘불편함’의 실체를 분석한다. 파레나스(Parreñas 2009)의 하향 탈구 개념을 전유하여, 주재원에 동반한 여성 배우자가 의도치 않게 태국 사회의 특권층 고용주 여성인 ‘마담’의 위치에 편입된 과정을 ‘상향 탈구’(upward dislocation)로 규정한다. 마담 까올리는 가사노동을 직접 하던 경험, 한국인·중산층·모성의 다층적 정체성이 태국 계급 구조와 충돌하며 내면화된 위치 감각과 성향의 시차(hysteresis)를 경험한다. 분석 결과, 불편함은 ‘매반’(가사노동자)의 신체·감각적 반응(눈·입·귀·몸)과 마담의 내면화된 성향이 달라붙고 엉키는 정동적 마주침이다. 마담 까올리의 권력은 매반의 시선(눈), 침묵(입), 소통(귀), 동선(몸), 노동(손길)과 엉키고 부딪치는 일상의 순간마다 느슨함과 팽팽함 사이를 오가며 아주 천천히, 그러나 분명하게 새어나가고 있었다.

  3. Singing Intersectionality : The practice of intersectionality seen through the women’s cultural movement organization “Unni-Network”
    Miyu MITSUTAKE · pp. 113-164
    摘要

    이 논문은 ‘교차성’을 핵심 키워드로 실천하는 여성주의 문화운동 단체 ‘언니네트워크’와 소모임 합창단 ‘아는 언니들’을 사례로, 그들의 어떠한 실천이 교차성 실천이며 그것이 어떠한 의미를 갖는지 분석한다. 언니네트워크는 영 페미니즘의 유산을 계승하며, 2015년 이후 심화된 퀴어 배제적 흐름 속에서도 퀴어페미니즘을 표방하며 차이를 배제가 아닌 연대의 동력으로 삼아왔다. 또한, 당시 페미니즘 운동이 단일 정체성에 집중하며 내부 소외를 낳기도 했던 것과 달리, 이들은 성별, 성적 지향, 연령, 노동자성 등 교차하는 정체성을 포괄하는 실천을 통해 위계 없는 수평적 공간을 구축한다. 특히 합창단 활동에서 이루어지는 ‘생활 나누기’와 가사 ‘편곡’ 작업은 개인의 고유한 경험을 언니들만의 언어로 재전유하며 정서적 지지와 해방감을 생산하는 핵심적 실천으로 기능한다. 본 연구는 안전한 공간 확보에 머물렀던 기존 논의를 넘어, 유머와 재미를 매개로 차이를 ‘음색의 다양성’으로 치환하는 복합적 활동에 주목한다. 이를 통해 교차성이라는 개념이 구체적이고 일상적 실천을 통해 어떻게 공동체의 지속 가능성을 담보하고 외부 세계와 연대 지점을 확장하는지 고찰한다.

  4. An Essay on the Ontology of the Gift : Gift-Giving as a Phenomenon of Name-Life
    Sejin PARK · pp. 165-222
    摘要

    『증여론』에서 모스는 증여에 대한 향후의 학적 논의를 지배하게 될 언어를 확립한다. 이 글은 그와는 다른 언어의 가능성을 타진한다. 이를 위해 우선 증여의 문제를 갚기가 아니라 주기, 갚을 의무가 아니라 줄 필요로 전환한다. 이어 ‘개체들 사이의 연결’이라는 통념으로 환원되지 않는 관계성들(뒤얽힘, 물림, 맞섬, 맞얽힘)이 증여에 연루되어 있음을 포틀래치를 중심으로 확인한다. 이 과정에서 ‘내-증여’와 ‘외-증여’의 구별을 도입해 그 분석적 가치를 탐색한다. 끝으로 줄 필요가 이름이 이끄는 삶 자체에 구성적인 것임을 논증하면서 증여를 생명(生名) 현상으로 정립한다.

  5. Transformation and Continuity of North Korean Arts after Migration : Repertoire and Performance of a North Korean Defector Performing Arts Troupe
    Haeeun SHIN · pp. 223-257
    摘要

    본 연구는 탈북민 공연예술단의 레퍼토리와 작품 분석을 통해 북한예술의 창작 원리와 특징이 이주 이후 새로운 사회적 맥락 속에서 유지 및 변형되는 양상을 살펴본다. 북한예술은 사회주의적 내용을 민족적 형식에 담는 원칙 아래 사상성과 인민성을 핵심 창작 원리로 하며, 통속성과 혼종성은 이를 구현하는 과정에서 나타나는 특징이다. 참여관찰과 심층면담을 바탕으로, 본 연구는 탈북민 예술단 공연에서 사상성은 소거되지만 관객의 정서와 미감을 고려하여 작품을 구성하는 인민성의 원리는 새로운 목적 아래 재편성되어 작동함을 밝힌다. 이들의 공연은 탈북민이 겪는 이산의 현실과 긴밀히 연결된 실천으로 한국 관객의 북한에 대한 정서적 간극을 완화하고 남북의 공존 가능성을 전달하려는 지향 아래 조직된다. 이 과정에서 예술단은 체화된 북한 무용과 노래의 표현 방식을 유지하는 한편, 한국대중문화 요소를 결합한 혼종적 작품을 창작한다. 본 연구는 이러한 현상을 단순히 이주사회의 신자유주의 체제 속에서의 문화적 적응이나 생계 활동으로만 설명하기보다, 북한예술의 창작 원리와 특징이 새로운 사회적 조건과 목적 속에서 변형·지속되는 과정으로 해석한다.

  6. Crossing the border with the “Spring Revolution” : The Transnational Lives of Myanmar Political Exiles in Mae Sot, Thailand
    Eunjeong LEE · pp. 259-285
    摘要

    2021년 2월 1일 미얀마 군부가 쿠데타를 선언한 후 시민저항을 기반으로 한 ‘봄의 혁명’이 진행 중이다. 군부의 탄압으로 인해 저항이 숨겨진 방식으로 전환되었고, 이를 피해 태국 매솟으로 피신하는 정치적 망명자가 증가하고 있다. 본 연구에서는 매솟으로 이주한 미얀마 출신 정치적 망명자가 어떻게 초국적 일상과 정치운동을 구성하는지에 초점을 맞춘다. 이들의 매솟으로의 이주는 단번에 이루어지기도 하지만, 정치 운동 와중에 생성된 네트워크를 통해 본국 내 소수종족 자치지역을 경유하는 방식으로 진행되기도 한다. 태국에서의 미등록 신분으로 인해 부조리한 임금과 처우를 경험하면서도 이들은 실시간으로 미얀마 내부의 상황을 접하고 국경을 초월한 정치적 연대와 저항의 흐름을 만들어낸다. 초국적 저항에서 본국, 체류국, 제3국을 연결하는 상호작용은 두드러지는데, 본국에서부터 습득한 기술로 매솟에서 영화를 제작해 제3국에서 상영하거나, 체류국 내 망명자 공동체에서 발생한 젠더 폭력 문제를 해결하고자 국제기구 및 지역 공동체, 양국과 제3국 정부와 연대한다. 초국적 지식 및 교육 활동은 국경을 넘어 본국과 제3국의 미얀마 공동체와 연결되면서 초국적 시공간 또한 확장되고 있다.

  7. Heritagization and Commodification of Korean Buddhist Temple Food : A Study of Devices of Valuation and Performative Infrastructure
    Junyong LEE · pp. 287-325
    摘要

    사찰음식은 최근 국가무형유산 지정과 상품화의 국면에서 보존해야 할 전통인 동시에 평가 및 유통의 대상으로 재배치되고 있다. 이 연구는 사찰음식과 관련 실천을 고정된 전통이나 상품 형식으로 환원하지 않고, 문서·지표·전문성·정통성 서사·노동·상품 형식이 결합하며 구성되는 수행적 인프라로 분석한다. 이를 위해 국가무형유산 지정과 사찰음식의 상품화에 관한 자료를 검토했고, 현장 사례인 S사찰 공동체를 대상으로 심층면담과 현장연구를 진행했다. 분석 결과, 국가무형유산 지정에서 동원되는 지표와 정통성 서사는 사찰음식의 가치를 중립적으로 발견하기보다, 특정 형식과 주체를 대표 전통으로 전경화하고 다른 실천과 기여를 배경화하는 가치화 장치로 작동한다. 상품화 또한 전통의 외부가 사찰음식을 훼손하는 사건이라기보다, 기존 전통 장치가 시장 장치와 접합하면서 관계의 형식을 재구성하는 과정으로 관찰된다. 그 과정에서 S 사찰 공동체의 공동-상품화 사례는 상품 형식을 활용하면서도 생산과 소비의 흐름을 공동체로 순환시키는 실험적 가능성을 보여준다. 결론적으로 사찰음식의 향방은 구체적 장치와 관계가 어떠한 배치로 수행적 인프라를 구성하는가에 달려 있다.

  8. Self-Explosion for Survival : Path Formation of the Koreanized Jiraikei and the Meanings of Embodied Self-Harm Practices
    Heejeong LIM, Sumin JUNG, Kwanwook KIM · pp. 327-366
    摘要

    본 연구는 2024년부터 2026년까지 수행된 온·오프라인 에스노그라피를 바탕으로, 한국형 ‘지뢰계’ 청소년들의 하위문화적 경로 형성과 자해 실천의 인류학적 의미를 규명한다. 한국형 지뢰계는 일본 서브컬처의 단순 모방이 아니라, 2015년 디시인사이드 우울증 갤러리 이후 국내 디지털 공간에 누적되어 온 자생적 우울계·자해계의 정동 계보가 일본의 시각적 양식을 전유하여 지역화된 결과물이다. 이들의 자해는 병리적 일탈이나 관심 끌기가 아닌 언어화되지 않는 구조적 고통을 관리하려는 체현된 행위자성의 표현이자 취약성에 기반한 감정 공동체의 의례적 연대 실천이다. 그러나 고통의 실재를 증명하려는 가시화 실천은 디지털 플랫폼의 불균형한 젠더 권력 구조와 결합하며 노골적인 성적 착취 및 도덕적 낙인의 회로에 포섭되는 역설을 낳는다. 본 연구는 단속과 규제 중심의 치안 담론이 청소년들의 고통을 해소하기보다 몸 내부로 은폐시켜 최근 약물과다복용(OD)계로의 더 위험한 변이를 유발함을 논증하고, 가시성을 대하는 사회적 태도의 근본적 전환과 공간 복지 및 플랫폼의 책임 구조 마련을 촉구한다.

  9. Open to All, Yet Not for Anyone : Value Commons at Jihyangzip in Jeonju
    Myong-suk JIN · pp. 367-410
    摘要

    이 연구는 전주에 위치한 지향집을 사례로, 커먼즈를 가능하게 하는 동력으로서 ‘가치’의 작동을 분석한 것이다. 공동체도, 공유 자원도 부재한 이 공간은 ‘무엇을 공유하는가’가 아니라 ‘어떻게 함께 살아가는가’를 통해 형성되는 커먼즈의 양상을 보여준다. 기존 커먼즈 연구가 커머닝의 과정과 제도적 조건에 주목해온 것과 달리, 이 논문은 사람들을 참여로 이끄는 내적 동력에 주목하고, 그 근거를 가치에서 찾는다. 지향집에서는 비건, 생태, 돌봄, 평화, 사회정의와 같은 가치가 낯선 타자와의 관계 맺기, 나눔과 협동의 실천을 지속시키는 힘으로 작동한다. 이러한 가치는 참여를 촉발할 뿐 아니라, 그 방식과 범위를 조정하고 관계의 윤리를 조직하며, 동시에 경계와 문턱을 생성하는 기제이다. 이 연구는 도시 커먼즈가 비물질적 영역으로 확장되고 있음을 밝히며, ‘가치 커먼즈’를 통해 커머닝의 동학을 재해석 한다.

  10. Ferrying ‘Americanness’ : An Oral Life History of Arirang Taxi Drivers at USAG-Yongsan
    Tae-Hyun HA · pp. 411-450
    摘要

    이 연구는 서울 용산 미군기지를 출입했던 아리랑택시 기사의 구술생애사를 통해 ‘미국적인 것’이 미군기지의 노동 조건 속에서 어떻게 경험되고 의미화되었는지를 분석한다. 선행연구는 미군기지에서의 한국인 경험을 기지촌 여성의 자기서사와 한국인 고용자의 동원과 노동 조건, 그리고 PX 블랙마켓의 경제사 분석을 중심으로 다뤄왔으나, 민간기업에 소속되어 기지 안팎을 매일 넘나들었던 택시 기사의 경험은 충분히 다뤄지지 않았다. 이에 이 연구는 건양기업 소속 아리랑택시 기사의 구술생애사를 통해 PX 블랙마켓과 달러 경제라는 비공식 경제 구조 속에서 구성된 ‘미국적인 것’의 경험에 초점을 맞춘다. 구술 분석 결과, 아리랑택시 기사는 PX 블랙마켓의 유통자이자 달러 경제의 참여자인 동시에 미군 폭력의 피해자라는 경계적 위치성을 지니고 있었다. 이들은 생존과 경제적 이윤을 위해 ‘미국적인 것’을 선택적으로 전유했으며, 낮은 임금과 달러 경제라는 구조적 조건 속에서 미국 문화에 대한 선망과 미군에 대한 적대가 교차하는 양가적 경험이 나타났다. 나아가 이 연구는 구술생애사가 위치성에 따라 냉전 경험이나 IMF 외환위기라는 역사적 국면이 달리 경험되는 방식을 포착하는 방법론적 도구임을 보여주며, 1980년대 이후 미군기지의 비공식 경제와 한국 사회의 관계를 밝힌다.

  11. Time, Knowledge, and Circulation : Rethinking Environmental Anthropology
    Yoon Jeong LEE, Hye yoon KWON, Eui Jin HWANG · pp. 451-502
    摘要

    이 논문은 시간, 지식, 순환이라는 키워드를 통해 환경 정치와 인류학의 새로운 가능성을 탐구한다. 먼저 시간의 정치학을 중심으로 고전 생태인류학의 단일한 시간 관념을 비판적으로 살피고 자본주의·식민주의가 야기하는 시간 갈등과 시간 주권의 문제를 고찰한다. 다음으로 지역 생태 지식, 존재론적 전회, 토착 이론을 교차시켜 자연을 이해하는 방식의 복수성과 그 이면의 권력 관계를 논증하며, 국내에 충분히 소개되지 않은 북미 토착 이론가들의 사유에 주목한다. 마지막으로 전지구적 상품 순환에서 필연적으로 생성되는 부산물을 중심으로 순환 개념을 재구성한다. 시간, 지식, 순환에 대한 논의들의 다성적 배치를 통해 이 논문은 인류학적 자산을 환경인류학 및 생태적 논의와 연계해 재독해할 가능성을 제시한다. 특히, 단일하고 연속적인 시간, 보편적이고 단일한 자연, 일방향으로 흐르는 사물의 순환이라는 전제가 환경 위기 속에서 흔들리는 가운데, 그 균열이 권력관계 속에서 불균등하게 분배되는 방식에 주목한다.

  12. The Petro-State and the Problem of Historicity : A Review of Ryan Cecil Jobson’s The Petro-State Masquerade
    Myeungpyo KOOK · pp. 503-524

Medicine Anthropology Theory

Vol. 10, No. 2 · 2026-06-22 · 10 篇 · Edinburgh U (open access) · Medical · Double-blind (verified)
  1. "Just Graphite": Corporate Representations of Particulate Matter in Santa Cruz, Rio de Janeiro
    Delia Rizpah Hollowell · pp. 1-24 · online 2026-06-22
    摘要

    The unevenly distributed environmental burdens of the Anthropocene become evident in conflicts surrounding the extractive industries. ThyssenKrupp’s steel mill (TKCSA) in Rio de Janeiro is an illustrative example. The factory transformed its surrounding landscape and emitted a fine metallic dust over its human and non-human neighbours. This article focuses on some of the less tangible elements of Anthropocene transformations around the mill. I examine ThyssenKrupp’s communication strategies to reveal the underlying meanings of corporate rhetorical devices, uncover the violence of public relations language and understand the intensity of feeling that surrounded it. I trace the affective registers that emerged around the steel mill as a result of its polluting activities, its approach to corporate communications, and its ‘corporate social responsibility’ (CSR) activities. Everyday life involved minimal corporeal expressions of emotion that encapsulated feeling and allowed for perseverance in the face of toxic suffering. The ‘Stop TKCSA’ campaign involved affective labour; emotions were the agentic contribution campaigners were able to make in the context of unequal power structures. I centre these less visible dynamics of power to examine how emotions can shape experiences of environmental conflict, form coalitional politics, and contribute to the very landscapes of the Anthropocene.

  2. Embodied Inequalities of the Anthropocene
    Jean Segata, Ceres Víctora, Paola Sesia, Laura Montesi, Jennie Gamlin, Sahra Gibbon · pp. 1-30 · online 2023-06-30
    摘要

    Introduction to the special issue 'Embodied Inequalities of the Anthropocene', guest edited by Jennie Gamlin, Laura Montesi, Sahra Gibbon, Paola Sesia, Jean Segata, and Ceres Victora.

  3. Situating Latin American Critical Epidemiology in the Anthropocene: The Case of COVID-19 Vaccines and Indigenous Collectives in Brazil and Mexico
    Laura Montesi, Maria Paula Prates, Sahra Gibbon, Lina R. Berrio · pp. 1-29 · online 2023-06-30
    摘要

    Diverse histories and traditions of critical epidemiology in Latin America provide an important, although underutilised, alternative framework for engaging with the embodied health inequalities of the Anthropocene. Taking COVID-19 as ‘a paradigmatic example of an Anthropocene disease’ (O’Callaghan-Gordo and Antó 2020) and drawing on ethnographic research in Brazil and Mexico on vaccination campaigns among Indigenous Peoples, we review and analyse the scope and limits of Latin American critical epidemiology in addressing Anthropocene health. While there are intersecting and parallel dynamics between diverse national and regional histories of epidemiology, we argue that the relatively differential focus on political economy, political ecology, and colonialism/coloniality in Latin American critical epidemiology, alongside the attention to non-western disease experiences and understandings, constitute a counterpoint to biomedical and specific ‘Euro-American’ epidemiological approaches. At the same time, Indigenous understandings of health/disease processes are intimately connected with territory protection, diplomacy with non-human entities, and embodied memories of violence. We examine how this presents new and challenging questions for critical epidemiology, particularly in how the ‘social’ is defined and how to address both social justice and social difference whilst also navigating the biopolitical challenges of state intervention in the era of Anthropocene health.

  4. Structural Vulnerability and Toxicity Experiences in the Uruguayan Soybeanisation Process
    Victoria Evia · pp. 1-28 · online 2023-06-30
    摘要

    Fuelled by agribusiness, transgenic soybean crops, genetically modified to withstand pesticide use, have increased in use during the last 20 years in the Southern Cone of Latin America. Plantations are understood as examples of ‘modular simplifications’ in ‘patchy Anthropocene’ landscapes (Tsing et al. 2019), where the attempt to reduce diversity may have social and ecological feral effects as diseases and toxins spread. In Uruguay, as an agro-exporter country, soybean expansionist processes correlate with an increased use of pesticides. Based on an ethnographic study (2016–2018) carried out in the main Uruguayan agricultural region, this Research Article seeks to analyse the experiences of toxicity among agricultural workers and rural inhabitants in the soybeanisation context. I propose that pesticide effects transcend biomedical diagnoses of ‘intoxication’. I also contend that the experience of toxicity can be understood as occurring along a continuum in the daily life of sufferers, which encompasses chemical and biological processes, their affects, intersectional conflicts, lay concepts of illnesses, informal self-care networks, and unequal access to health services. This ethnography demonstrates that the experience of toxic suffering embodies inequalities in environmental health in the time of the Anthropocene and is shaped by structural vulnerabilities and politics of exposure.

  5. Sustaining (Dis)Embodied Inequalities in the(ir) Eurocene: Ancient Microbes, Racial Anthropometry, and Life Choices
    Coll De Lima Hutchison, Andrea Núñez Casal · pp. 1-33 · online 2023-06-30
    摘要

    Racialisation and colonialism are central to sustaining (dis)embodied inequalities. We bring together our distinct ethnographic projects to explore this. The first project accompanied a microbiome expedition involving Amazonian Indigenous non/human communities, whereas the second project focussed on medical professional’ encounters with Mbya Guarani communities in the Atlantic Forest region. Both projects explore racialised assumptions of human difference and colonial extractive practices. In the case of medical intervention with the Mbya, and their forms of life, this is perpetuated through the imposition of anthropometric growth standards. With the human microbiome initiatives, identifying Indigenous Peoples as potential reservoirs for novel probiotics also ultimately amplifies racialised (dis)embodied inequalities. Rather than these interventions addressing such disembodied equalities, we draw parallels between the two, to show that they perpetuate them. Finally, we propose that part of ceasing to reproduce these (dis)embodied inequalities requires ‘us’ to challenge the racialised and colonial histories of the life and geological sciences, to recognise their embodied consequences in the present, as well as how they are implicated in emergent proposals for new geological ‘-cenes’.

  6. The Plantation as Hotspot: Capital, Science, Labour, and the Earthly Limits of Global Health
    Alex Nading · pp. 1-26 · online 2023-06-30
    摘要

    Central American sugarcane plantations have become ‘hotspots’ of chronic kidney disease (CKD). Although CKD is frequently caused by diabetes or hypertension, most sugarcane plantation workers who have it have no history of either condition. They are among a growing number of people worldwide afflicted by chronic kidney disease of nontraditional causes (CKDnt). The ‘hotspot’ concept resonates with those who study CKDnt in part because work-related heat stress has been the factor most strongly associated with the disease. Drawn from ethnographic research on CKDnt in Nicaragua and a close reading of scientific and policy documents, this research article frames the plantation as a health hotspot in three ways. First, plantations are sites of intensive, environment-altering capital investment. Second, public health research on the connection between heat stress and CKDnt remakes plantations as sites of intensive experimental scrutiny. Third, plantations produce innovative genres of political action. Nicaragua’s epidemic has given rise to a form of health activism, or ‘hotspotting’, in which workers and their allies use digital media to circulate evidence of environmental harm. This tripartite view of plantations as hotspots highlights what I suggest are some of the ‘Earthly limits’ of global health.

  7. Toward a Broader View of Health in the Anthropocene: The COVID-19 Syndemic and the Clash of Cosmographies in Mato Grosso do Sul, Brazil
    Raquel Dias-Scopel, Daniel Scopel, Esther Jean Langdon · pp. 1-28 · online 2023-06-30
    摘要

    The continual expansion of developmental frontiers has impacted dramatically upon Indigenous health in Brazil. When the COVID-19 pandemic struck in Mato Grosso do Sul, its Indigenous populations were already living in circumstances of environmental degradation, food insecurity, racism, and structural violence. The synergistic interaction between the SARS-CoV-2 virus, other pathogens, and biosocial factors resulted in what Singer (2010) terms as ‘syndemics’. In the case of Mato Grosso do Sul, it brought about a substantial increase in the disease burden of Indigenous Peoples, where child malnutrition, obesity, hypertension, respiratory and parasitic diseases, and maternal mortality appear at higher rates than in the non-Indigenous population. This Research Article discusses the coping and participatory strategies that were employed by Indigenous Peoples early in the pandemic. Efforts by Indigenous Peoples to address the pandemic reveal ‘a clash’ between Indigenous and Colonial cosmographies with regard to notions of the body and health. Considering the Indigenous perspective on the relation between territoriality and health, the analysis highlights asymmetries of power and embodied vulnerabilities and the limits of the Anthropocene as a global perspective.

  8. Toxic Legacies and Health Inequalities of the Anthropocene: Perspectives from the Margins
    Melania Calestani · pp. 1-29 · online 2023-06-30
    摘要

    Based on research in Matamoros (Mexico) and Naples (Italy), this article critically deconstructs embodiments and social histories of toxicity, addressing uneven power relations and health inequalities generated through late capitalism of the Anthropocene. By focusing on food and water consumption in regions at the margins of borders and defined as toxic wastelands, it sheds light on the multidimensional power structures of the global economy. Whether through reference to the illegal dumping of toxic waste by mafia-structured organised crime groups, or the contamination caused by foreign-owned assembly plants, the article illustrates how mechanisms taking place in the individual realm are ‘subsumed’ (Breihl 2019, 33) within more complex historical, economic, and environmental processes. These two case studies are the point of departure to reflect upon the undisclosed but powerful impact that commercial determinants of health have on individuals’ wellbeing, feeding profound north–south inequalities.

  9. Unruly Waters, Unsanitary Bodies Abject Terrains, Rehabilitation, and Infrastructures of Dispossession on the U.S.–Mexico Border
    Carlos Martinez · pp. 1-27 · online 2023-06-30
    摘要

    Drawing on ethnographic fieldwork among homeless deportees living in the Tijuana River canal, I examine how the ‘rehabilitation’ of toxic terrains can have corporeal and social consequences for those inhabiting such spaces. For decades, the Tijuana River basin traversing the U.S.–Mexico border has been perceived by officials from both countries as an unruly body of water. Prone to persistent flooding, the canal also experiences flows of toxic sewage from Tijuana’s maquiladora industry. In recent years, the riverbed in Tijuana has been inhabited by homeless and drug using communities, many of whom have been deported from the U.S. In response, rehabilitation of the canal and forced drug rehabilitation have been conjoined and promoted by the state as solutions for managing this unruly terrain and its residents. I take the deployment of the term ‘rehabilitation’ targeting both homeless deportees and the canal as an opportunity to consider how the concurrent disciplining of landscapes and human populations has been a central and evolving feature of the Anthropocene. I examine how my homeless interlocutors have experienced ‘rehabilitation’ as a violent process of abjection, dispossession, and captivity, which has converted this transborder landscape structure into a carceral zone under the guise of urban sanitation and health promotion.

  10. Wixárika Practices of Medical Syncretism: An Ontological Proposal for Health in the Anthropocene
    Jennie Gamlin · pp. 1-26 · online 2023-06-30
    摘要

    By understanding a community’s medical system, we are able to see its body ontology and how the people within it live in relation to the world, a historically constructed ideological position. Modernisation and development have restructured Indigenous communities and devalued traditional ontologies, including medical systems. This is a global pattern, where historical power relationships defined the coloniality of being and from this, organised healthcare, governance, and education in relation to patriarchal and capitalist universals. These social structures underlie the Anthropocene geological epoch and planetary crisis. Wixárika Indigenous communities live a polytheistic sociality; their medical system treats the spiritual origins of illness, attending to social cohesion in a society of humans, the supernatural, flora and fauna. This system is subalternised by dominant universals of biomedicine, which treat the body as separate from the environment and society. I refer to this epistemological inequality as the ontological Anthropocene. Wixaritari use both allopathic and traditional medical systems, following a non-hierarchical syncretic understanding of wellbeing. Giving equal importance to both systems may be a framework with implications for wellbeing beyond human health. This Research Article proposes that by centring Indigenous sociality that is more-than-human we can reconceive our planetary relationships in the broadest sense.

Anthropological Quarterly

Vol. 99, No. 3 · 2026-06 · 7 篇 · George Washington U · Sociocultural · Author-anonymized review (verified)
  1. "Our Church is Changing Gitanos for the Better": Masculinity, Stereotypes, and the Christian Moral Order in the City
    Antonio Montañés Jiménez · pp. 393-423
    摘要

    Based on long-term ethnographic research among Roma/Gitano Pentecostal communities in Madrid, Spain, this article examines how Pentecostal Gitano men articulate their moral engagement and struggles within the urban context. It provides an ethnographic account of how rhetoric, archetypes, and references to storytelling—including biblical Christian gendered principles and prevailing heteronormative notions of manliness in barrios—shape the construction of urban Gitano masculinities. The article contributes to the expanding literature on the intersection of masculinity and Christianity, specifically investigating how public expressions of masculine piety and virtue relate to urban experiences, ethics, and reflexivity. Rather than merely documenting behavioral changes, the analysis centers on how Spanish Pentecostal Gitano men narrate profound transformations in their relationships with other Gitano men, their inner emotional lives, and the urban environments they inhabit, following spiritually transformative encounters with the Holy Spirit. A substantial portion of Pentecostal Gitano narratives concerning moral cultivation and masculine moral behavior is articulated through holistic Christian concepts such as los frutos del Espiritu Santo (the fruits of the Holy Spirit). Specific fruits, including amor (love), docilidad (gentleness or docility), and dominio propio (self-control), serve as key idioms that render moral cultivation intelligible, meaningful, and valued within communitarian and institutional Gitano contexts. This article advances scholarship on urban religious masculinities by analyzing how dominant cultural stereotypes inform the construction of Christian public personae in settings characterized by asymmetric cultural power relations, and by linking the emergence of masculine moral and redemptive themes to Christian moral apologetic traditions that emphasize Christianity's potential to reconstitute social orders and generate societal benefits through the proliferation of Christian churches.

  2. Killing the River to Build the Border: Ecological Violence, the Rio Grande, and the US-Mexico Border
    Chilton Tippin · pp. 425-459
    摘要

    This article advances ecological violence as a theory to describe multiple forms of violence along the Rio Grande in the El Paso, Texas-Cd. Juarez, Chihuahua borderlands. Ecological violence denotes a modality of violence entangling people, more-than-human beings, and the enlivened places they co-produce. This concept complements other theories of violence applied in the US-Mexico border context while insisting that structural harm tethers to land-and-water relations, discursive erasures, and white settler colonialism. I ground these claims in ethnographic research conducted along the Rio Grande in 2024. Fieldwork included multiple longform interviews with a key interlocutor at Ysleta Del Sur Pueblo—a tribe whose ceremonial relationships with the river have been obstructed by the border wall and other infrastructures of exclusion and surveillance—as well as participant engagement with first responders and migrant advocacy groups. In 2024, the El Paso Sector became the deadliest zone for migrants along the entire US-Mexico border, with drowning a leading cause of deaths. These drownings occur as the US has hardened the border, drawing the Rio Grande's waters into a series of lethal, moat-like canals. This article thus argues that building the border entails a gradual killing of the river, as well as the disruption of Indigenous lifeways and systematized migrant deaths—all of which illustrate ecological violence's presence in the borderlands.

  3. "The Spiritual Elevator": Ritual Efficacy in Russian Orthodox Prayer
    Stephen Pax Leonard · pp. 461-485
    摘要

    This article explores the tension between mystical prayer and immutable, ritual speech. The tension derives from the fact that mysticism resists linguistic formulation. In the context of embodied, psychosomatic prayer, long-term fieldwork shows what brings about the efficacy of such petition. "Felt" intimacy with God is a slow process and is aided by "sincerity" and "embodying the word"; "sincerity" comes about through the pointedness of the intentionality conveyed by the words. As a public-private practice, the Jesus Prayer keeps crossing boundaries between ritual, everyday socialization, and hesychastic isolation. It is this permeability which makes it such a compelling ethnographic study. Such research clarifies the relationship between repetitive, conventionalized acts of piety and its effects on the supplicant because it shows that language is not diminished by formalization or repetition as has previously been implied.

  4. The Corporeal Humanity of the Kashmiri Militant: Body, Image, Song
    Mohamad Junaid · pp. 487-520
    摘要

    "Kashmiri militant" has been a historically opaque, shape-shifting figure in the Indian nationalist imagination. Represented as a threatening symbolic impurity within the national body politic, the Kashmiri militant is seen variously as dangerously nihilistic, nonpolitical, and incomprehensibly violent; impressionable yet uncompromisingly death-deserving; and embodying an unassimilable Muslim masculinity (in contrast to a desirable Kashmiri femininity). In the visual culture of the Indian occupation in Kashmir, police and military photographers circulate pictures of dead Kashmiri militants, disheveled and bloodied with torn clothes and limbs out of joint, presenting them as wild, hunted felons. For Kashmiris, however, who mourn and memorialize their death, the Kashmiri militant is a polyvalent personhood narrated as bridegroom, child, lover, saint, warrior, and martyr. Based on visual counterculture centered on and inaugurated by Burhan Wani, a young militant whose death led to weeks-long protests in 2016, this article examines the political lives and afterlives of the figure of the "militant" in Kashmir. Situating Wani's images within the broader history of violence against Kashmiri bodies, the article argues that the "Kashmiri militant," as a spectral presence in the Indian colonial-nationalist imagination and as an embodiment of moral resistance to Indian control in Kashmiri memory, defies a neat conceptualization of which bodies deserve humanistic consideration. I argue that the militant body as death-deserving is the effect of how the Indian state symbolically and violently polices the borders of nationalism and humanity, as well as of the liberal calculus of innocence and guilt.

  5. Against Citation
    Louisa Lombard · pp. 521-535
    摘要

    There has been a major change in anthropology and related disciplines that has curiously received little attention. Until about 1960, many of the best anthropology books and articles had no bibliography. Today, a single journal article might cite one hundred references. The academics who think citation is a problem generally argue for citing more, especially by taking into consideration the authors' identities. But citation-centric writing changes how we think. It makes us insular and afraid; it speeds the decline of empirical truth and the rise of theory-as-truth; and it prevents us from experiencing the joy and discovery that have been among our field's hallmark contributions. RÉSUMÉ: Un changement majeur s'est opéré en anthropologie et dans les disciplines connexes, lequel a, curieusement, suscité peu d'attention. Jusqu'aux environs de 1960, bon nombre des meilleurs ouvrages et articles d'anthropologie ne comportaient aucune bibliographie. Aujourd'hui, un simple article de revue peut citer une centaine de références. Les universitaires qui perçoivent la pratique de la citation comme un problème plaident généralement pour une citation plus abondante, en veillant tout particulièrement à prendre en compte l'identité des auteurs. Or, une écriture centrée sur la citation modifie notre façon de penser. Elle nous rend insulaires et craintifs; elle accélère le déclin de la vérité empirique au profit de la « théorie érigée en vérité »; et elle nous prive de cette joie et de ce sentiment de découverte qui figurent parmi les contributions emblématiques de notre discipline.

  6. Rethinking Sovereignty from the Commons
    pp. 537-548
  7. Artery: Racial Ecologies on Colombia's Magdalena River by Austin Zeiderman (review)
    pp. 549-555

한국민속학 (The Korean Folklore)

Vol. 83 · 2026-05-31 · 7 篇 · 한국민속학회 · 민속학 · Peer-reviewed (double-blind not verified)
  1. Diversity in the Transmission of Saengjeonyesujae and Shifting Evaluation Criteria
    Teahan Hong · pp. 7-29
    摘要

    이 글은 생전예수재 전승의 다양성을 바탕으로 무형유산을 바라보는 가치 기준의 변화가 필요함을 제시한 글이다. 현지 조사를 통해 생전예수재 설행 양상을 살핀 결과 비핵심 구성 요소인 설단, 장엄을 충실하게 구현하고 범패와 작법무를 알차게 설행하는 생전예수재가 있기도 하고, 가시적인 설단과 장엄을 겨우 갖추고 범패와 작법무도 소박하게 갖추거나 갖추지 않은 생전예수재가 존재하기도 한다. 이처럼 다양한 생전예수재의 설행 양상은 생전예수재 설행 사찰의 규모, 경제력과 긴밀한 연관이 있다. 생전예수재는 두 가지 방향을 보인다. 하나는 사회 변화의 흐름을 수용하여 비핵심 구성요소에 상당한 변화를 주면서, 신도들의 참여와 본래의 목적을 구현하는 대부분의 생전예수재이다. 설단과 장엄을 사정에 맞게 변화시키고 어산 스님들의 규모도 조정하여 생전예수재의 외형은 갖추지만 화려함은 갖추지 못한다. 이것은 민속이 적응력을 보여주는 사례가 된다. 다른 하나는 생전예수재의 원형을 구현하는 모습이다. 온전하게 설단과 장엄을 갖추고, 어산 스님이 중심이 되어 범패와 작법무를 격식에 맞게 구현하면서, 생전예수재가 가지고 있는 가치도 구현하고, 생전예수재가 가지고 있는 다양한 예술성을 보이는 것이다. 전자 생전예수재는 사회 변화를 수용하여 간략하게 설행하는 것으로 민속의 변화상을 보여주는 사례가 되지만, 무형유산 제도에서는 긍정적인 평가를 받기 어렵다. 후자 생전예수재는 공연성과 예술성을 잘 드러내는 의례로 현재의 무형유산제도 아래에서는 긍정적인 가치 평가를 받을 수 있다. 생전예수재의 본질적인 가치에 주목한다면, 사회 변화를 수용하여 비핵심 구성 요소는 두드러지지 않지만, 사회 변화를 수용한 생전예수재의 가치도 인정되어야 한다. 이러한 생전예수재가 오히려 민속의 변화를 보여주면서, 민속 수용층의 민속에 대한 인식을 잘 드러내기 때문이다. 전승의 다양성을 바탕으로 가치 평가 기준의 변화가 필요하다고 한 것이 이 글의 핵심이다.

  2. The Heterogenous Transformation of Oikeios and Assemblage of Performance Folk
    Seok-min Kang · pp. 33-68
    摘要

    이 글은 연행민속의 유산화 혹은 유산화된 연행민속의 문제를 물질 · 기술 · 환경이 얽힌 관계생태의 전환 과정이라는 자리에 놓고 다시 읽기를 시도한 것이다. 이러한 시도는 텍스트와 상징에 주로 초점을 맞춘 기존 연행민속 내 물질 연구가, 근/현대성이 정초한 새로운 환경 속에서 연행이 성립 또는 변형되는 물질적 조건과 비인간 행위자의 역할을 충분하게 설명하지 못하는 데다 오히려 그것을 인간 행위자에 수반된 것으로 해석하는 경향에 대한 대안적 관점의 일환이라고 할 수 있다. 이 글은 전통적인 세시풍속 내지는 공동체민속의 연행성을 지역 문화유산이라는 맥락에 따라 다소간 유지하면서도, 현대적 물질과 기술 인프라의 개입속에서 그 자신의 존속을 도모하고 있는 것으로 판단되는 옥천 교평마을 강줄당기기를 분석 대상으로 삼는다. 자본세 혹은 세계생태론적 견지에서 볼 때 한국사회의 여느 농촌과 지역이 그런 것처럼, 해당 사례가 자리하고 있는 관계생태 역시 점차 인간/자연(과 사회/자연)의 구분 관념에 따라 인공적인 것이 지배적인 경향으로 재편되어 가고 있다는 점을 비판적으로 탐독하는 가운데, 그에 따라 개발 · 생산 또는 도입된 사물과 물질들이 오히려 연행의 시간성과 공간성, 감각과 정동을 적극적인 차원에서 구성하는 행위자가 되고 있다는 점에 주목하고 또 천착하고자 했다. 아울러, 그러한 양태를 이른바 ‘유산의 탈구’로 규정해보고, 이것을 이른바 원형으로부터의 단절이나 이탈을 표방하는 지표로 간주하기보다는 관계생태의 혼종적 재편에 따른 연행민속의 새로운 배치로 접근해보았다. 물론 중요한 것은, 연행을 성립하게 하는 가능성이 근본적으로 인간 자연과 비인간 자연이 얽히는 관계 자체, 이른바 ‘다발적 행위주체성’에 의한 것임을 명확하게 주지하는 일일 것이다. 그리하여 이 글은 궁극적인 차원에서 물질의 창발과 사물의 행위성이 개입하는 것으로 연행민속을 다시금 규정하고, 나아가서는 이것이 근/현대성의 작동을 비판적으로 사유하는 하나의 대상이자 방법이 될 수 있음을 지적하고자 했다.

  3. The Mythological Development of Samdugumibonpuri and its substance
    Tae-hyo Kwon · pp. 69-97
    摘要

    이 글은 제주도 특수본풀이의 하나인 〈삼두구미본풀이〉의 성격을 밝히는 한편, 괴물 형상의 삼두구미 정체를 밝히고자 마련한 글이다. 이를 위해 인물 성격, 사건의 전개, 제의적 면모 등 여러모로 유사한 양상을 보이는 함경도의 〈산천굿〉 무가와 비교하면서 〈삼두구미본풀이〉의 성격을 파악하고자 했다. 아울러 제주도의 이장 방식에 대한 조사 보고를 바탕으로 삼두구미와 관련된 제의의 성격 및 삼두구미의 정체에 접근하고자 했다. 〈삼두구미본풀이〉는 자료 성격상 가) 삼두구미 자료를 제주도로 한정하지 않고 시야를 넓힐 대상임과 나) 이장 의례와 관련되나 부의 획득 면모도 보인다는 점, 다) 무속적 기반이 왕성한 제주시나 동부보다는 서남부에서 주로 채록되어 필수적인 무속의례로 진행되지는 않았을 것, 라) ‘삼두구미팔각신’이라는 명칭이 보이듯 그 형태에 대한 인식이 뚜렷하지 않은 점, 마) 이장 풍습을 담고 있음에도 현장에 대한 의례적 측면의 접근을 소홀히 한 점을 파악하였다. 다음으로는 함경도 〈산천굿〉무가와 인물의 설정 및 성격, 사건 전개 양상 등의 비교를 통해 〈삼두구미본풀이〉의 모호했던 부분을 파악했다. ‘대맹이-삼두구미’, ‘붉은 선비-나무꾼’, ‘영산각시-셋째 딸’ 등 중요 인물이 서로 대비되며 맞닿아 있다. 무능하면서 스스로 만든 고난을 지혜로운 딸에게 전가하는 나무꾼, 지혜를 통해 괴물을 물리치고 부를 획득하는 역할의 셋째 딸, 대맹이에 대응하는 사람을 해치고 괴롭히는 괴물 삼두구미는 퇴치되어 땅에 가루로 뿌려져 동티를 일으키는 존재여서 의례적인 처리가 필요한 대상이 됨을 인물 비교를 통해 연계할 수 있었다. 사건의 전개 양상은 가) 금기의 부여와 금기 어김, 나) 괴물의 출현 및 괴물의 요구, 다) 여주인공의 지혜로운 행적을 통한 죽을 위기 모면, 라) 괴물의 퇴치 및 처치 과정, 마) 결과 및 의례의 기원 등의 모습을 보이면서 상호간 밀접한 관계 양상을 보이며, 궁극적으로 이장이나 산천 지기와 관련된 동티를 막고자 하는 의식과 관련이 있는 결말임을 알 수 있다. 한편 〈삼두구미본〉이 이장 관련 무가임에도 실제 현장 조사 자료를 소홀히 한 측면이 있어 진성기의 조사 내용을 가져와 삼두구미와 이장 의례와의 연계성을 제시하였으며, 특히 〈산천굿〉이 산천 동티를 막는 성격이듯 〈삼두구미본〉 또한 이장의 동티를 막는 의례로 보았다.

  4. The Handdeutgye of Hwanghae-do Shamans as a Heritage Community
    Seong Hee Oh · pp. 99-130
    摘要

    이 글은 인천에서 황해도 출신 구대인 만신들이 조직한 ‘한뜻계’를 무형유산의 풀뿌리 공동체적 참여 사례로 분석한다. 한뜻계의 기반에는 한국 민속에 있어 주요한 공동체 형태인 계(契)가 있었다. 한뜻계는 분단으로 지리적 기반을 상실한 황해도 실향민 만신들이 지역 정체성을 공유하며 결속한 친목계다. 약 30년간 지속된 한뜻계 안에서 황해도 만신들은 개별 활동의 경계를 넘어 상호 연대했고, 이후 뱅인영감굿의 전승 주체로서 공동체적 참여를 실천했다. 따라서 이 글은 한반도의 분단과 도시화라는 사회적 변화 속에서, 동계 중심의 ‘마을공동체’ 이상을 넘어 무형유산 전승 기반으로서 친목계가 지니는 의미를 고찰한다. 특히 한뜻계는 무속이라는 동일한 생업을 지닌 사람들이 계라는 조직을 통해 사회 민속적 관계를 맺은 사례다. 이를 통해 한국 무형유산 제도 변화 속에서 등장한 ‘전승공동체’ 개념의 새로운 가능성을 제시하고자 한다. 그동안 유네스코 협약의 ‘공동체’ 개념과 한국의 ‘전승공동체’ 개념 사이의 간극이 논의되어 왔다면, 한뜻계의 사례는 이 둘을 연결할 수 있는 실천적 가능성을 보여준다.

  5. Local Conflicts and Disputes over the Production and Transformation of Chunhyang’s Portrait
    Mok-Hwa You · pp. 131-164
    摘要

    춘향 영정은 춘향제의 정체성을 구성하는 핵심 상징임에도 불구하고, 그 변천 과정과 이를 둘러싼 사회적 갈등에 대한 학술적 조명은 미흡한 실정이다. 이에 본 연구는 춘향 영정의 역사적 변천 과정을 고찰하고, 2023년 새 영정 봉안을 둘러싼 논란과 핵심 쟁점을 심층적으로 분석하는 데 목적이 있다. 이를 위해 문헌 및 신문 자료 분석과 더불어 참여관찰과 면담 조사를 병행하여 춘향 영정이 시대별 권력 구조와 정책적 요구 속에서 어떠한 방식으로 재현되어 왔는지를 추적하였다. 연구 결과, 춘향 영정의 재현 주도권은 춘향제 태동기의 기생 및 지역 유지에서부터 일제강점기 식민 관료, 군사정권, 그리고 오늘날의 문화 행정에 이르기까지 지속적으로 변화해 왔음을 확인하였다. 또한 2023년 새 영정 제작 이후, 해당 영정이 춘향의 형상과 정신을 충분히 구현하지 못했다는 비판이 제기되면서 절차적 정당성을 둘러싼 논쟁이 본격화되었다. 이 과정에서 ‘최초 영정 복위’ 측과 ‘새 영정 재제작’ 측 입장 간의 춘향 상징의 해석을 둘러싼 견해 차이가 뚜렷하게 드러났다. 그럼에도 양측 모두 춘향을 남원의 대체 불가능한 문화 자산으로 인식하고 있으며, 각자의 진정성을 사회적으로 인정받고자 한다는 점에서 공통점을 보였다. 이러한 분석은 공공 상징물의 제작과 재현 과정에서 민주적 합의와 지역사회 참여가 충분히 작동하지 않을 경우 갈등이 심화될 수 있음을 보여준다. 따라서 향후 영정 논란의 완화와 실질적 해결을 위해서는 이해당사자들이 의견을 조율할 수 있는 공론화 절차의 제도적 마련이 필요하다. 아울러 시민 의견이 실제 정책 결정 과정에 일정하게 반영될 수 있는 참여 구조의 설계도 요구된다.

  6. Cultural politics of reciprocity toward indigenous ecological capabilities －Focusing on the case of the Hongdong area －
    Youngbae Lee · pp. 165-212
    摘要

    지역에서 지식문화 생산과 향유의 주체화가 주목된다. 이는 일과 놀이가 분리되지 않고 융합된 특징을 보였던 민속사회의 문화적 연행과 향유 방식과 닮아있다. 이 글은 이와 같은 지역의 특이성에 주목하여 그 실천 지형과 양상을 관련 행위자들의 구술기억에 의거하여 재현하고자 했다. 활동가들은 실천과 담론의 통합적 행위자성을 강조하고 있다. 실제로 그들의 담론적 실천이 다양한 방식으로 이루어지고 있다. 이러한 조건에서 매개와 재현 작업을 통해 이루어지는 기존 접근 방식의 전환이 필요하다. 이 필요에 부응하여 이 글은 실험 · 체험 · 기억 · 해석 · 논평이 함축된 행위자들의 구술기억을 다중화하는 재현 방식을 취하고자 했다. 지역에서 생태적 가치는 매우 근본적인 측면에서 실천되고 있다. 그것은 삶과 사회, 세계와 우주를 관통하고 통합하는 전일적인 사유와 실천 양식으로 지향되고 있다. 실천가들은 생태적 가치를 마음의 양식 또는 삶의 양식으로 전환할 수 있어야 한다고 확신하며 그 실천 양식을 만들어가고 있다.

  7. Dragon-Palaces and Waterworks － Modern Infrastructure and the Reconfiguration of Spirit-Human-Material Relations －
    Haklak Choi · pp. 213-239
    摘要

    이 연구는 상수도라는 근대적 인프라의 작동이 물 생산의 수단-겸-행위자인 용신과 인간의 관계에 야기한 변화를 고찰한다. 전근대 사회에서 우물은 물 생산의 도구와 용신의 신체(神體)라는 두 기능이 복합된 원초적 인프라로 작동했고, 그중 물 생산의 도구로 사용 가치가 신체로서 숭배 가치에 선행했다. 상수도 인프라의 도입과 함께 자연은 점차 생산의 주체에서 객체로 전환됐고, 수단과 행위자가 분리됐으며, 우물을 매개로 하던 용신과 인간 사이 접촉과 생물학적 연결의 고리가 소실됐다. 마을신앙의 현장에서 전승되는 우물제사의 신체는 이러한 조건에서 나타나는 용궁의 분기와 변형의 궤적을 예시한다. 형태는 그대로이지만 물 생산 기능을 상실하거나, 형태는 변했지만 물 생산 기능을 지속하거나, 형태만 모방되고 물 생산 기능이 소거되는 등의 전개이다. 무속의 현장에서 역시 유사한 변화의 궤적에서 오래된 용궁이 재활용되거나 새로운 용궁이 생성된다. 다만 특정 장소와 공동체에 기초한 마을신앙의 우물제사와 달리, 오직 무속을 위해 분화된 상업적 공간에서 기도의 시설로 증식하는 용궁은 전국 불특정 다수의 무당에 의해 제사될 수 있다. 어느 편이든 이제 우물은 원초적 인프라로 기능하지 않고, 숭배 가치가 사용 가치에 선행하는 원초적 예술의 형식으로 전환된다. 이 원초적 예술의 생산에는 물 생산과 소비 사이 사회적 관계의 맥락이 소실된 복합적 사회에서 부상하는 새로운 진정성에 대한 욕구가 반영되어 있고, 그 욕구를 충족하는 ‘사실성의 아우라’를 창출하기 위해 전근대적 우물 즉 용궁의 원본이 모방된다. 용신에게 물 생산의 기반을 의탁한 삶의 양식이 더 이상 일상의 현실이 아니게 되었기 때문에 덧붙여져야 하는 사실성의 아우라다.

Modern Asian Studies

Vol. 60, No. 2 · 2026-03 · 12 篇 · Cambridge UP · Asia (history & society) · Double-blind (verified)
  1. Political notes of nauras : Emergence of an aesthetically embodied kingship practice in Adil Shahi Bijapur
    Namrata B. Kanchan · pp. 263-289 · online 2026-07-17
    摘要

    From late sixteenth century, Bijapur’s sultan, Ibrahim Adil Shah II (r. 1580–1627 ce ), used nauras —a polyvalent term used extensively within the Sanskrit aesthetic realm—on his kingdom’s cultural artefacts as well as political and administrative insignia. An avid music lover, he is renowned for his Dakani work on music titled Kitāb-i Nauras ( circa 1580s). Portrayed by previous scholars as religiously unorthodox, Ibrahim Adil II and nauras are located chiefly within narratives of Bijapur’s political instability and the sultan’s indulgence in leisure over statecraft. Recent scholarship has challenged such notions but no study focuses on the reasons for nauras ’ crossover to the political domain or its appearance on state insignia. This article offers new insights by locating the sultan within a matrix of Indic, Turco-Mongol, and Islamicate sovereignty practices. In doing so, it argues that Ibrahim Adil II strategically employed the corporeal, religious, aesthetic, and polyvalent dimensions of nauras to forge a uniquely personalized embodied cosmic kingship practice to govern a multicultural populace. In particular, this article elucidates that the realms of art, aesthetics, and kingship were not mutually exclusive in the Indic or Islamicate worlds and that nauras was a rational manoeuvre to elevate the ruler above courtly factions and religious differences.

  2. Whose women and whose honour? A fragile imperial discourse on the Baluch-Punjab frontier, late nineteenth century
    Gagan Kumar · pp. 290-322 · online 2026-07-06
    摘要

    This article focuses on the Punjab-Baluch borderland region during the last decade of the nineteenth century. It shows how this region was a conflict zone, not only between local communities across borders but also between the two colonial frontier governments of Punjab and Baluchistan. The article explores the nature of these conflicts by examining a cross-border dispute involving women. It analyses how different competing legal systems—Shariat, customs, and colonial law—operated on the ground and were used by the disputants to resolve the conflict. Additionally, it discusses how, during this process, the colonial state documented women’s presence and used their voices and actions to serve the objectives of state-making. Through this case study, the article demonstrates that in local feuds involving women, colonial authorities emerged chiefly as disputants rather than just arbitrators. By claiming to be the legitimate custodians of the ‘abducted’ women, the two frontier governments competed against each other to fulfil the aspirations of the tribal communities they represented. This intra-tribal and intra-colonial dispute reveals deep fissures in colonial understanding of questions of state intervention, sovereignty, and colonial subjecthood—rendering the imperial project inconsistent, fragile, and contradictory in the Punjab-Baluch borderlands.

  3. Typhoid, milk, and the military: India’s first dairying revolution, 1880–1925
    David Arnold · pp. 323-346 · online 2026-09-04
    摘要

    Government alarm over the extent of enteric fever (typhoid) among British troops in India in the late nineteenth century focused concern on adulterated milk as a likely medium for the transmission of disease and incentivized the creation of cantonment dairies, of which there were more than 30 by 1914. Aided by the introduction of modern milking techniques and butter-making equipment, military farms and government-sponsored dairies helped create a new market for dairy products in India, fostered the growth of privately run dairies, and encouraged the idea of milk consumption as an aid to health. The new dairies, with their intensive cattle-breeding programmes, were further seen as model institutions and centres for the dissemination of scientific dairying across India. The cantonment dairies drew in part on India’s own cattle culture and anticipated the ‘white revolution’ of the 1970s; but the uniqueness of the cantonment environment, the high costs of intensive dairying, and the reliance on specialized breeding inhibited the wider application of the military dairy model while intensifying Hindu opposition to cantonments as cow-killing sites.

  4. Borderland extraterritoriality: The Yunnan-Burma frontier meetings (1902–1949)
    Li Wan · pp. 347-372 · online 2026-09-22
    摘要

    Britain exercised a distinctive form of extraterritorial jurisdiction around the Yunnan-Burma border before 1949. In 1902, Chinese and British officials established a joint trial system under the 1902 Manai Agreement. From then on, Chinese, British, and Burmese officials convened temporary frontier meetings each winter in border areas, including in Manai, Nawngma, and Sima. These meetings adjudicated disputes involving theft, injuries, and trans-frontier cultivation pursuant to tribal customary law. The frontier meetings normally issued four distinct types of judgments and awarded a uniform remedy in the form of damages. This system served the practical needs of border tribes by delivering swift justice to prevent blood feuds and maintain law and order. These meetings developed a case law for resolving borderland cases that diverged from the national laws of both China and Britain. This system was jointly administered by Chinese, British, and Burmese officials under ostensibly reciprocal terms, though Britain’s exercise of extraterritorial jurisdiction in China ultimately placed a heavier burden on the Chinese side. The joint trial system came to an end after Burma officially gained independence in 1948. British extraterritorial jurisdiction in China was not absolute but operated through distinct jurisdictional systems in the interior and in the borderlands.

  5. The brokerage of order in Karachi
    Adeem Suhail · pp. 373-392 · online 2026-08-05
    摘要

    This article reframes Lyari’s gangs in Karachi as brokers of political order rather than merely criminal entities. Drawing on five years of ethnographic and archival research, it argues that the figure of the ‘gangster’ functioned as a political intermediary, stabilizing contradictions between military regimes, capital accumulation, and working-class urban life. Through portraits of three interlocutors—a tragic and unlikely ‘gangster’, a gang underboss who mediated between state projects and the denizens of his neighbourhood, and a socialist activist targeted for elimination by the gangs on behalf of the state—the article demonstrates how state, civil society, and scholarly discourses have unwittingly colluded to produce ‘Lyari-as-gangland’ without attending to what the gangs actually did in Lyari apart from the violence that overdetermines their era of dominance. It argues that coding brokerage as criminological masks the gang’s everyday functions in projects of public health, housing, and bureaucratic mediation. Furthermore, the rise of gangs to this role is historically situated in the conjuncture of the role of Pakistan’s military dictatorship in the global war on terror, where violent non-state actors were instrumentalized to produce a highly unequal order in the city.

  6. Waves of feeling: Emotions, media, and the Chinese Communist Revolution
    Dayton Lekner · pp. 393-415 · online 2026-08-11
    摘要

    How did the Chinese Communist Party (CCP) foster emotional engagement with its revolution? How did the advent of new media transform this engagement? Building on research that has noted the importance of emotions in the CCP’s revolution, this article explores these questions through a study of CCP radio and the person of the announcer. Using broadcast circulars and directives, memoirs, and diaries, it explores how broadcasters found an appropriate emotional tone for political texts, and how individual broadcasters functioned as nodes in the diffusion of emotions over an expanding network of radio stations. I make three arguments: first, that along with the well-documented normative forces of Party organization, announcers relied on reason and introspection to find their own emotional expression of a text and, with that, an emotional engagement with the revolution; second, that announcers were not anomalous in their reasoned engagement, but represent a consistent strain of community-building emotional practice within the ranks of the CCP; and third, that the advent of radio empowered the CCP to scale up its subject-oriented emotion work, developed at Yan’an but locally bound, to the regional and, eventually, the national scale. In this scaling up, extant CCP work-practice interacted with the promises and limits of the new media to create a distinct broadcasting culture that would shape the use of radio throughout the Mao era.

  7. Managing culture: The role of music in state-led cultural reproduction in post-colonial Pakistan
    Ghulam Ali Shair · pp. 416-448 · online 2026-09-17
    摘要

    If music’s place remains ideologically contested in the national cultural formation of post-colonial Pakistan, what are the terms, if any, by which the state engages with this art form? Answering this question, along with others, this article advances the term ‘logic of managing culture’ to explore the Pakistani state’s relationship with music. Through contesting India-centric scholarship on music and cultural politics in the South Asian context and relying on an eclectic array of empirical evidence, I argue that the post-colonial state’s engagement with music is marked by a set of priorities that entails a logic of managing music rather than reproducing it. This orchestration plays out at two major levels: the international, involving strategically projecting selected elements of Pakistan’s musical-cultural identity internationally, and the national, relying on language (Urdu) and religion (Islam) for national integration, while bypassing the role of music. I suggest that this amounts to a logic of cultural management, wherein the state, through a set of cultural institutions, ‘conducts’ music and its practitioners while presupposing the ideologically contested place of this art form in the national cultural formation of Pakistan.

  8. ‘The state won’t stop us’—Hate-crime adjudication under the SC/ST Atrocities Act in India
    Prashant Iyengar · pp. 449-518 · online 2026-07-06
    摘要

    This article presents conclusions from a study of 1,400 criminal trials in cases involving anti-caste and tribe violence, filed in the trial court of Aurangabad District (Sambhajinagar) in western India. Employing computational analysis upon the metadata and text of decisions filed under the Scheduled Castes and Scheduled Tribes (Prevention of Atrocities) (POA) Act 1989 during a 20-year period (2002–2023), I offer an exploratory socio-legal analysis of hate-crime adjudication in India. This article sheds light on hitherto unexplored questions about criminal adjudication in India such as: Which cases are most likely to result in acquittals and convictions? What kind of evidence does the court consider and in what manner is evidence read? How frequently are cases dismissed due to witnesses turning hostile? What kinds of punishments do Indian courts hand out? How long do criminal trials typically last? What kind of charges are most commonly brought against the accused in these cases? How easy is it to get bail and what conditions do courts typically impose? How long do POA-accused spend in jail before they get bail?. In doing so, this article offers a first-of-its kind phenomenology of ‘hate-crime’ adjudication and, more broadly, of criminal adjudication in India.

  9. The dynamics and ambiguities of ‘Chineseness’: Interethnic marriage practices in the Dutch Batavia before 1850
    Victoria S. Zhou, Tak-Wai Hung · pp. 519-544 · online 2026-09-16
    摘要

    This article explores how interethnic marriage produces and governs ambiguous identities within legally pluralist port-city settings. Taking the construction of ‘Chineseness’ in the Dutch Batavia before 1850 as a case study, this article analyses how identity was contested, negotiated, and reconfigured in a colonial legal landscape. Drawing on archival records from the Batavia Kong Koan (the Chinese Council), this article argues that normative orders from the homeland, such as Confucian ethical distinctions between the ‘civilized’ and the ‘uncivilized’ and gendered prescriptions governing family life and female conduct, were not merely adapted to local conditions but were frequently disrupted by diasporic realities. Through case studies of interethnic marriages and female-initiated divorces, the article highlights how female agency within the Kong Koan’s hybrid legal regime fractured the ideological coherence of the Confucian moral order and exposed the instability of ‘Chineseness’ under colonial rule. Situated at the intersection of claimed Chinese custom, Dutch colonial statutes, and the everyday realities of interethnic co-residence, the Kong Koan did not preserve tradition so much as mediate and fragment it. Rather than reinforcing a fixed notion of identity, the institution became a mechanism for its continuous remaking. By locating identity within everyday social practice and the institutional logics of colonial governance, this article challenges essentialist conceptions of diaspora. It offers a historically grounded account of how an ethnic identity like ‘Chineseness’ was continuously unmade and remade at the intersection of multiple legal and cultural systems within colonial jurisdictions.

  10. Inscribing chölkha : Census, election, and the making of modern Tibetan diaspora identity
    Ugyan Choedup · pp. 545-584 · online 2026-09-21
    摘要

    This article examines the historical formation of modern chölkha identity, a cornerstone of contemporary Tibetan nationalist thought. Rather than a direct continuation of medieval Tibetan identity categories, this article argues that modern chölkha identity is a contingent product of the Tibetan encounter with colonial modernity, especially in exile. By analysing the introduction of the modern census and electoral representation in exile, this article demonstrates how institutional logics of governmentality reconstituted once-fluid, elite-defined spatial categories into standardized, mutually exclusive popular identities. This is not to suggest that chölkha identity was invented ex nihilo by exiled Tibetan nationalist elites. On the contrary, and well known among scholars, the chölkha , as a spatial category among the literate class, has existed since the thirteenth century. Yet, as this article argues, its form, scope, and semantic load were different from those of its modern counterpart. Drawing on this history, the article proposes a ‘sequential thesis’ as a way of reframing Benedict Anderson’s concept of seriality. This article argues that ‘unbound’ and ‘bound’ serialities are best understood not as opposing types but as historical sequences, a process wherein fluid imaginaries are contingently captured by the state’s institutional grid, generating the rigid collective subjectivities that define the modern world.

  11. Hybridity, glocalization, and the negotiation of contemporary Chineseness in guoman (national animation): White Snake: Afloat (2024)
    Chenglong Zhou · pp. 585-612 · online 2026-09-22
    摘要

    This article examines Light Chaser’s animation White Snake: Afloat (2024) within the context of the revival of Chinese guoman 國漫 (national animation) since the 2010s. It reflects on a key gap in existing literature: a failure to analyse the dynamic interplay of state ideology, market demands, and animators’ agency in the construction of specific guoman texts. Drawing on an interdisciplinary framework comprising Homi Bhabha’s ‘hybridity’, Roland Robertson’s ‘glocalization’, and Weiyu Zhang’s ‘Chineseness as identity work’, this article explores how the tripartite negotiation leads to distinct forms of hybridity across three aspects of the film—techno-artistic, narrative, and structural—against the backdrop of media glocalization. The analysis also examines how the film’s hybrid forms embody competing versions of Chineseness. The overall argument is that the hybridity in Afloat is a product of the triad’s complex negotiation; it illuminates the contentious status quo of contemporary Chineseness as a globalized ‘identity work’—a dynamic, ongoing, and contested process of constructing identity, encompassing not only a subject’s self-identification but also how others identify that subject.

  12. Allies apart: China, Russia, and the limits of mnemonic friendship
    Vincent K. L. Chang, Eric S. Zhang · pp. 613-641 · online 2026-09-17
    摘要

    Addressing a gap in memory studies and international relations, this article examines the construction and limits of Sino-Russian mnemonic friendship through historical narratives of the Second World War. Drawing on extensive multilingual datasets of Chinese and Russian state-sponsored media output, supplemented by additional primary sources and fieldwork in China, it identifies a complex pattern of selective remembering and strategic forgetting across four key wartime themes: the wartime Kuomintang (KMT)–Chinese Communist Party (CCP) united front, early Soviet aid, bilateral tensions in Xinjiang, and the Soviet offensive in Manchuria. While Beijing and Moscow’s official discourses converge on the moral lessons of the war, alignment on historical events remains largely confined to strategic silences. There is little substantive narrative convergence, and personal wartime stories and Sino-Soviet cooperation remain largely untapped. This limited convergence is explained by domestic legitimation imperatives, ethnonationalist impulses, enduring mutual distrust, and divergent visions for regional and global order. The article argues that mnemonic alliances are inherently fragile, dependent on bottom-up engagement and broader social resonance, even in authoritarian contexts. Drawing attention to the mnemonic dimension of China–Russia relations illuminates the fragility of mutual trust and the long-term vulnerability of this relationship, as narrative silences can be easily undone.

  1. Experience, person, and myth: A socio-cultural history of the ‘oily man’ in post-colonial Malaysia and Singapore
    Esmond Chuah Meng Soh, Jin Rong Aw · pp. 1-29 · online 2026-10-26
    摘要

    Drawing on English, Chinese, and Malay newspapers, this article examines the ‘oily man’, a figure who shifted between sexual assailant, ghost, and intruder across twentieth-century Singapore and urban and semi-urban peninsular Malaya/Malaysia. It situates him not as a timeless ‘Malay’ entity but as an elastic label and repertoire, assembled and reassembled through rumours, retellings, and recurring events. Departing from most studies of Malay-world monsters, which reduce such figures to allegories or cinematic symbols of gender, modernity, or social change, this article demonstrates how the oily man’s historical force lay in intrusions, panics, and community responses—events mediated, disputed, and reworked across time and place. His history is framed through three registers: experience, person, and myth, which trace how this elastic label took shape through encounter, attribution, and retelling. Experience captures sightings, rumours, and assaults that animated fear; person traces how individuals were accused or apprehended as oily men; myth designates the narratives and repertoires that amplified these encounters in film, fiction, and folklore. Across these registers, the article shows how monsters emerge through lived circulations of testimony, attribution, and storytelling. It highlights the value of historical research that privileges experience and offers a framework for future scholarship: monsters as unstable, context-specific composites of encounter, narrative, and transmission that illuminate the entanglements of state, society, culture, and religion in post-colonial Malaysia and Singapore, with resonance for the study of rumour and monstrosity elsewhere in Asia.

  2. Where is Rama in Bengali folk songs? A case of devapolitik, mythopoetic dissonance, or performative abstention in bhakti federalism of Bengal
    Sayantan Thakur · pp. 1-16 · online 2026-10-26
    摘要

    This article examines the striking marginality of Lord Rama in Bengali-language folk songs and performance-based devotional repertoires, reading this absence not as a simple omission but as a culturally patterned silence shaped by regional aesthetics, vernacular religiosity, and historical power formations. Rather than presuming a uniform pan-Indian bhakti field, the article focuses on Hindu devotional song-worlds in Bengal—especially Vaishnava-inflected oral and performative contexts—where Krishna emerges as the dominant affective deity: intimate, plural, and theatrically adaptable. Rama, by contrast, appears as comparatively distant, associated with moral restraint and epic discipline that often resist the emotional grammar of folk performance. Drawing on folkloristics, performance theory, and critical approaches to discourse and cultural hegemony, the article argues that devotional absence can function as a mode of cultural agency: what is not sung may still carry ideological weight. Concepts such as devapolitik, mythopoetic dissonance, bhakti federalism, performative abstention, and scriptural decentring are deployed as interpretive tools to illuminate how regional devotional cultures selectively remember, transform, or sideline divine figures, thereby producing a distinctive devotional geography within Bengal’s plural religious landscape.
